16. Handling timeouts: 


when calling a remote service, you want to have a limit in terms of how long we're willing to wait to get a response. We don't want to wait forever. So let me show you how to implement this scenario. I'm going to create a completable future, supply async, now, here we want to simulate a slow connection. So, long task.simulate, eventually we expect to get a result like 1. It doesn't really matter what the result is. So, this returns a completable future. Now, this future object has a method called or timeout. And with this we can enforce a limit. Here we need to pass two arguments, one is a timeout value, let's say 1, and the other one is a time unit. This is an enumeration, so here we can say we want to wait 1 second. Now, this will return a new completable future that we timeout after 1 second. So if we call the get method to get the result, we're going to get an exception. Let's wrap this inside a try catch block, okay? Now we get the result and store it over here, and finally print it. Let's run the program. So after one second, our program crashed, we got an exception of type execution exception, which is caused by a timeout exception, okay? Now, this is not an ideal experience for the end user. a better approach is to recover with a default value. So, instead of calling or timeout, we're going to call complete on timeout, here we need to pass three values, the first one is the default value that we're going to return if this task times out. So we're going to return 1, the second is our timeout value, once again 1, and finally a time unit. So time unit.seconds. this will return a new completable future that will complete after 1 second. So, let me reformat the code, that is better, now let's run the program again, this time after 1 second, we got our default value. This is a better experience for the end user. 




Codes and Other Notes Referred in this Discussion:


CompletableFuturesDemo.java: 

package executors;

import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;
import java.util.concurrent.ExecutionException;
import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;

public class CompletableFuturesDemo {
    public static void show(){
        var future = CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            LongTask.simulate();
            return 1;
        });

        try {
            var result = future.orTimeout(1, TimeUnit.SECONDS)
                    .get();
            System.out.println(result);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException(e);
        } catch (ExecutionException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException(e);
        }

    }
}




Main.java: 

import executors.CompletableFuturesDemo;

public class Main {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        CompletableFuturesDemo.show();
    }
}

with completeOnTimeout:

CompletableFuturesDemo.java: 

package executors;

import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;
import java.util.concurrent.ExecutionException;
import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;

public class CompletableFuturesDemo {
    public static void show(){
        var future = CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            LongTask.simulate();
            return 1;
        });

        try {
            var result = future.completeOnTimeout(1, 1, TimeUnit.SECONDS)
                    .get();
            System.out.println(result);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException(e);
        } catch (ExecutionException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException(e);
        }

    }
}


Main.java: 

import executors.CompletableFuturesDemo;

public class Main {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        CompletableFuturesDemo.show();
    }
}

1

Quote.java:

package executors;

public class Quote {
    private final String site;
    private final int price;

    public Quote(String site, int price) {
        this.site = site;
        this.price = price;
    }

    public String getSite() {
        return site;
    }

    public int getPrice() {
        return price;
    }

    @Override
    public String toString() {
        return "Quote{" +
                "site='" + site + '\'' +
                ", price=" + price +
                '}';
    }
}




FlightService.java:

package executors;

import java.util.Random;
import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;


public class FlightService {
    public CompletableFuture<Quote> getQuote(String site) {
        return CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            System.out.println("Getting a quote from " + site);

            LongTask.simulate();
            var random = new Random();
            var price = 100 + random.nextInt(10);

            return new Quote(site, price);
        });
    }
}

19. Solution - Getting Many Quotes: 

Now that we have built this small piece of functionality, we can work on getting quotes from different websites. So back to our flight service class, we have the get quote method, now let's add a method called get quotes. So public, what should be the return type of this method? We want to return a list of completable future of quote. So, list of completable future of quote. let's import the list class, now we call this get quotes, now this method should have the knowledge of the websites you want to query. So, let's store them in a list of strings, here I'm going to use list of, and pass site 1, site 2, and site 3, now, for each site we should get a quote, there are two ways to do this, one way is to create a list of completable future of quotes, iterate over these sites, get each site for each site, call the get quote method, and then add the result to our list. But there is a better way, we can use functional programming techniques, we can use the streams API. So, we're going to get a stream from this list, and then map each object, or each string to a completable future of quote. So we get the site, and here we call get quote with that site. This lambda expression is simply calling a method so we can convert it to a method reference. That's better. Now what is the return type of this map method? Take a look. x, we press f1 here, so this returns a stream of completable future. So earlier we talked about intermediate and terminal operations in streams. The map method is not going to execute anything, it's just going to build a pipeline. It's going to build a recipe for us. So eventually we need to collect the results into a list object, so here we call collect, and pass collectors.toList, so this will return a list of completable futures, and then we'll return it from this method. Now, let's test our new method, so we go to our demo class, instead of calling the get quote method, we're going to call get quotes, now this returns a list of completable futures for each future we need to consume it and print its result which is the quote object. So once again we can use the streams API here, we convert this list to a stream, and then we use the map method. So here we should pass a function that takes a completable future of quote and returns something else. So we're going to take a future object and then call then accept, and here we need to pass a consumer of quote. So this is where we get the quote object and print it on a terminal. So once again we can use a method reference, quote double colon to string. Now, once again we're building a processing pipeline, so nothing is going to happen until we use a terminal operation. So here we can call collect, and then collect the result into a list. Now there is something redundant here. In our flight service, after we call the map method, we get a stream, we get that stream and then collect the result into a list. Now, in our demo class, once again, we get that list and convert it back to a stream, this is unnecessary. So in this method we don't really need to collect the result into a list, let's delete this line, now we should return a stream of, let's import this, a stream of completable future of quote, okay? Now we don't need this import statement either, So our method is cleaner, we can even put this on the same line, nice. Now back to our demo class, we don't need to call the stream method anymore, so we get the quotes, this will give us a stream of future objects, for each future object, we'll accept the result and print the code. Now actually I made a mistake here, instead of calling quote the toString, we should call the print line method. So we should pass this quote object that we get the print line method. So, system.out double colon print line. Now, let's run our program and see what happens. So we're querying all these websites at the same time, after 3 seconds, they all respond, beautiful.

package executors;

public class Quote {
    private final String site;
    private final int price;

    public Quote(String site, int price) {
        this.site = site;
        this.price = price;
    }

    public String getSite() {
        return site;
    }

    public int getPrice() {
        return price;
    }

                "site='" + site + '\'' +

FlightService.java:

package executors;

import java.util.List;
import java.util.Random;
import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;
import java.util.stream.Stream;

public class FlightService {
    public Stream<CompletableFuture<Quote>> getQuotes() {
        var sites = List.of("site1", "site2", "site3");

        return sites.stream().map(this::getQuote);
    }

    public CompletableFuture<Quote> getQuote(String site) {
        return CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            System.out.println("Getting a quote from " + site);

            LongTask.simulate();
            var random = new Random();
            var price = 100 + random.nextInt(10);

            return new Quote(site, price);
        });
    }
}






CompletableFuturesDemo.java: 

package executors;

import java.util.stream.Collectors;

public class CompletableFuturesDemo {
    public static void show(){
        var service = new FlightService();
        service.getQuotes()
                .map(future -> future.thenAccept(System.out::println))
                .collect(Collectors.toList());

        try {
            Thread.sleep(10_000);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            throw new RuntimeException(e);
        }
    }
}

20. Solution - Random Delays: 

Now let's make this more interesting. I'm going to add a random delay to each website. I want each website to respond at a different time. With this you can see that we're displaying these quotes as we get them. let's go to our long task class, I want to overwrite the simulate method, and in the second version, I want to introduce a parameter called delay. So instead of 3 seconds we're going to use this argument. Now we go to our flight service class, we're going to reuse our random object to pass a random delay to the simulate method. So let's say we want each website to respond between 1 to 3 seconds, we have a baseline of 1 second, plus random, that next int, 2 seconds. Now let's run a program and see what happens. So we're querying this concurrently, and we're getting responses in different times, beautiful. Now the last part, we want to print a final message to say how long it took to query all these websites. That is pretty easy, so we go to our demo class, After we get the quotes, we collect them in a list, let's store the result over here, this is going to give us a list of future objects, let me show you. So, list of completable future of void. Now we can use the all of method to wait for the completion of all these future objects. So, completable future dot all of, here we have a var args parameter, technically this is a syntactic sugar over arrays, so we can pass an array of completable future objects. That is pretty easy, we call futures, the two array, now this method is overloaded, the version without parameters returns an object array, let me show you, I'm going to put this on a new line, take a look. So the version without a parameter returns an object array, we don't want this because the all of method expects an array of completable future objects. Okay? So we should use the second version and here we should pass an array of completable futures. With this, we'll get a completable future array. So, over here we pass new completable future of 0. So we pass an empty array, and with this, we're telling this method that we want the result to be a completable future array. Now, we wait for all the result, let me put this on a new line, pay attention to how I'm formatting this code. So we have completable future, we wait for all these futures to complete then we're going to run this piece of code. This is where we're going to print our final message. First we need to calculate how long it took to execute this operation. So just before we start we're going to get the current time, we can use the local time class, this class has a static method called So this returns the current time. Now, over here, we're going to repeat this. So, we calculate the end time, local time.now, then we create a duration object. So this duration class has a static method called between, we can pass the start and end time, and get a duration object. And finally we print a message like, retrieve all quotes in here we call duration.2ms, this will return the duration in ms, and finally we add msec. Okay? Let's run the program and see the result. Codes and Other Notes Referred in this Discussion:

LongTask.java:

package executors;

public class LongTask {
    public static void simulate() {
        try {
            Thread.sleep(3000);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
    }

    public static void simulate(int delay) {
        try {
            Thread.sleep(delay);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
    }
}





Quote.java: 

package executors;

public class Quote {
    private final String site;
    private final int price;

    public Quote(String site, int price) {
        this.site = site;
        this.price = price;
    }

    public String getSite() {
        return site;
    }

    public int getPrice() {
        return price;
    }

    @Override
    public String toString() {
        return "Quote{" +
                "site='" + site + '\'' +
                ", price=" + price +
                '}';
    }

FlightService.java: 

package executors;

import java.util.List;
import java.util.Random;
import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;
import java.util.stream.Stream;

public class FlightService {
    public Stream<CompletableFuture<Quote>> getQuotes() {
        var sites = List.of("site1", "site2", "site3");

        return sites.stream().map(this::getQuote);
    }

    public CompletableFuture<Quote> getQuote(String site) {
        return CompletableFuture.supplyAsync(() -> {
            System.out.println("Getting a quote from " + site);

            var random = new Random();

            LongTask.simulate(1_000 + random.nextInt(2_000));

            var price = 100 + random.nextInt(10);

            return new Quote(site, price);
        });
    }
}






CompletableFuturesDemo.java: 

package executors;

import java.time.Duration;
import java.time.LocalTime;
import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;
import java.util.stream.Collectors;

public class CompletableFuturesDemo {
    public static void show() {
        var start = LocalTime.now();

        var service = new FlightService();
        var futures = service.getQuotes()
                .map(future -> future.thenAccept(System.out::println))
                .collect(Collectors.toList());

        CompletableFuture
                .allOf(futures.toArray(new CompletableFuture[0]))
                .thenRun(() -> {
                    var end = LocalTime.now();
                    var duration = Duration.between(start, end);
                    System.out.println("Retrieved all quotes in " + duration.toMillis() + " msec.");
                });

        try {
            Thread.sleep(10_000);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            e.printStackTrace();
        }
    }
}






Main.java: 


import executors.CompletableFuturesDemo;

public class Main {
    public static void main(String[] args) {
        CompletableFuturesDemo.show();
    }
}




Output:
Getting a quote from site1
Getting a quote from site2
Getting a quote from site3
Quote{site='site1', price=100}
Quote{site='site3', price=109}
Quote{site='site2', price=103}
Retrieved all quotes in 2951 msec.
